Proposition 1 (complete construction): for a full-dimensional convex 3-polytope P with a vertex v whose incident facets are all triangles, there is a spanning tree of original edges to cut along plus a nonconstant analytic motion on [0,eps) with every facet rigid and lying on the boundary of the convex hull of the moving facets — applies to every simplicial convex 3-polytope (apices' copies move initially in outward-normal directions; support-margin derivative sij'(0)=1-ni·nj>0). Proposition 2 (exact second-order-locked tetrahedron): A=(0,0,0), B=(1,0,0), C=(0,1,0), D=(-1,1,1), cuts along AB, AC, CD — first-order cone CT nonzero but combined support margin Phi has DPhi(0)=0 and second derivative -1, so no nontrivial local motion for that cut tree; NOT a counterexample to the original problem (this tetrahedron satisfies Proposition 1 under different cuts) — the cut-tree-vs-polytope distinction is indispensable. Precise remaining obstruction: unproved that for every P there exists a dual spanning tree T with S_T(P) containing a nonconstant continuous path from q=0; even the weaker first-order universal assertion not established, and Proposition 2 shows first-order integrability can fail, so proving that alone would not finish the problem. Literature note: question located as Problem 9 attributed to Bob Connelly, OWR 2006 printed page 693; Demaine et al. Continuous Blooming of Convex Polyhedra (2011) and O'Rourke-Vilcu Skeletal Cut Loci do not resolve the exact original-edges-only hull-preserving local question. Reply attached Python script verify_locked_tetrahedra.py. Reply 1 headline verbatim: "I have not obtained a complete necessary-and-sufficient characterization." Partial results reply 1: (1) Theorem 3 — for every p greater than 0 a fixed trigonometric-polynomial forcing admits infinitely many positive periodic solutions whose minima tend to zero, so "a lower bound min x ≥ c(f,p,T) greater than 0 cannot be proved in the stated generality—not even when f is analytic"; explicit p=1 example: f(t) = (3/4)(1−cos t)^2 (1+3cos t), limiting profile X(t) = (1−cos t)^{3/2}. (2) Theorem 1 — explicit upper bound max x ≤ [(p+1) T D / (4 p (1 − (P/N)^{(p+1)/(2p)}))]^{2/(p+1)} with P = ∫f+, N = ∫f−, D = N−P; gives compactness of solutions bounded away from zero, but "The restriction x ≥ ε cannot be made uniformly over all positive solutions". (3) Theorem 2 — "an exact convex reformulation": positive solutions are exactly the extreme points of C_f = {u greater than 0 periodic : u'' − α u'^2/u ≥ g}; C_f nonempty iff ∫f less than 0, but "Nonemptiness is easy; extreme-point existence is the unresolved part of this approach". Literature notes: Gasull's problem uses f not identically zero; Ureña's "Periodic solutions of singular equations" proves sign change + negative mean sufficient for p ≥ 2 with nondegenerate zeros; Ureña's later "A counterexample for singular equations with indefinite weight" gives a C^∞, sign-changing, negative-mean forcing with no positive periodic solution for p = 5/3 — "Consequently, the two elementary necessary conditions are not sufficient in general."; the chat "did not find a later complete resolution." Reply 2 headline verbatim: "I have not obtained the unrestricted necessary-and-sufficient characterization." Partial results reply 2: Theorem A — compactness survives collapse in variable u = x^{p+1}: "there is no additional measure supported at the zeros of u"; Corollary B (p ≥ 1): "every sign-changing, negative-mean forcing has a nonnegative generalized periodic solution" (NOT a positive-solution theorem). Theorem C — "weighted-divergence criterion" giving a complete characterization within a stated class (f with finitely many zeros, p ≥ 1, and at every zero adjacent to f greater than 0, ∫₀^δ f(t₀+εs)/s^p ds = ∞ on at least one side): then "x^p x'' = f has a positive periodic solution iff max f greater than 0 and ∫₀^T f less than 0" — but "this is a complete characterization within the stated class, not for arbitrary continuous f" and "the condition is not necessary in general" (the reply-1 collapsing family has positive solutions despite failing it). Theorem D — solvable forcings lie arbitrarily close on both sides of any admissible f, so "Increasing the forcing preserves existence" and "Decreasing the forcing preserves existence" both fail; "even arbitrarily accurate approximation and two-sided bracketing by solvable equations do not establish solvability of the prescribed equation". Theorem E — for 0 less than p less than 1 an extra necessary condition "−(1/T)∫₀^T f less than (2p/(1−p)) max f" (eq. 22), yielding an explicit analytic nonexistence family: for 2p/(1+p) ≤ c less than 1, "x^p x'' = cos(2πt/T) − c has no positive T-periodic solution" (eq. 24), for every 0 less than p less than 1 — but it "does not establish the converse for this cosine family". Final status verbatim: "Gasull's full Problem 9 is not resolved by the results above." Precise remaining obstruction: extreme-point existence for C_f — whether generalized nonnegative periodic solutions can be chosen strictly positive, i.e. the zero-contact problem. | 159 | 30003818 / OWR-16164-012 | Brownian First-Visit Cell Lengths on the Circle | 0.2057 | 5.0 | 3 | 2018 | claimed_solved | 1/5 |  | Complete joint Laplace law for every finite k>=1 of independent continuing Brownian walkers on a normalized circle, arbitrary fixed distinct labeled seeds and independent-uniform or equidistant seeds. Explicit finite permutation sums and deterministic exit-kernel integrals give each coefficient, with cumulative physical clocks, a uniform factorial outer remainder and compact-simplex moment determinacy. Earlier painting and classical methods credited: Gomes Júnior–Lucena–da Silva–Hilhorst (1996); Miller (2013); Chatterjee–Nabahi–Terlov (2026); Baccara (2026); Si (2026); Fitzsimmons–Pitman (1999); Régnier–Dolgushev–Redner–Bénichou (2022). No named density, fast algorithm or certified inner quadrature. Finite-model and scalar diagnostics supplement the written proof; they do not prove the Brownian theorem. The dated bounded audit found no prior complete general target law; no absolute/exclusive priority or independent discovery is established. Gomes1996/Dicker2006 fulltext403 and Zenodo registry403 remain limitations; reopen priority on a concrete general-resolution source. New results: prime-power duality lifting (PD_n(F_p) implies duality over Z/p^r for all r, orientability a separate character check); p-adic orientation characters constraining top integral homology (B[p^infty] is 0, Prufer, or Z/p^{e_p}); common integral fundamental class exists iff H_n(G;Z) = Z (cd_Z(G)=n suffices); explicit E_0 telescope model showing fieldwise duality does not synchronize integral fundamental classes (not a group counterexample). Remaining obstruction unchanged: PD_n^+(Q) + PD_n^+(F_p) for all p => FP_{ceil(n/2)}(Z) still unproved, the unbounded-exponent torsion class epsilon in H^{m+1}(G;T) still uneliminable. 5th reply in progress at check time. 2026-09-28 ~00:00 PDT (row365-msg5-check): read-only check of reply 5/5 (complete, 'Worked for 51m 20s'). Opening verdict verbatim: 'I have not reached a full resolution.' New: (1) conditional splitting criterion in dimension 3 — under PD_3^+(Q) + PD_3^+(F_p) for all p PLUS the extra assumption cd_Z(G) = 3, integral orientable PD over Z is equivalent to splitting the extension 0 -> K -> H^3(G; ZG) -> Z -> 0 as ZG-modules (K = ker tau_c, uniquely divisible, a QG-module), equivalently vanishing of e_G in H^1(G; K); splitting as abelian groups is automatic and insufficient; does not cover cd_Z(G) = 4 and does not prove e_G = 0. (2) explicit simply-connected space X = mapping telescope T_p of degree-p maps (H~_2 = Z[1/p]) with a 4-cell attached via the Hopf map: the same integral class c gives Poincare duality over every field and every finite coefficient ring, but integral cap product in the middle degree is the zero map — shows coefficient-theoretic data cannot replace the missing finiteness argument; NOT a counterexample to the group problem (not aspherical; pi_1(X) = 1). Unchanged: PD_n^+(Q) + PD_n^+(F_p) for all p => [PD_n^+(Z) iff FP_{ceil(n/2)}(Z)]. Final verdict verbatim: 'This remains a partial investigation, not a full resolution of Problem 21.70.' No full resolution; no escalation. Verbatim key claims saved to goal hidden_files/row365_reply5_2026-09-27_keyclaims.txt. Row closed UNSOLVED at 5/5; slot freed and refilled with row 20. |  |
